<TD NOWRAP VALIGN="bottom"><I>%&nbsp;</I></TD></TR>
</TABLE> <P STYLE="margin-top:8pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">In US$ million, unless noted otherwise. EBITDA = Operating income + Depreciation of PP&amp;E + Depreciation of the right of use
assets + Amortization of intangible assets + Unproductive exploratory drillings + (Reversal) / Deterioration of PP&amp;E. Adjusted EBITDA = EBITDA that excludes IFRS 16 effects +/- <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">one-off</FONT> items. Net Leverage
Ratio = Net Debt / LTM Adj. EBITDA. FCF = Cash flow from Operations less CAPEX (Investing activities), M&amp;A (Investing activities), and interest and leasing payments (Financing activities). Fuels = diesel + gasoline. R&amp;M is refining and
marketing business, it excludes petrochemicals and agro products. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:8pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Adj. EBITDA</B> totaled US$4,654&nbsp;million in <B>2024</B> (+15% y/y), mainly
driven by local fuel price recovery, growing oil export revenues and shale oil expansion, coupled with lower fuel imports (volume&nbsp;&amp; unit cost) and better tariff in Metrogas; partially offset by local fuel demand contraction (especially
diesel) and higher unit cost of oil purchased to third parties in our refineries. Also, 2024 performance includes around <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(-US$300</FONT> million) from mature fields and <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(-US$85</FONT>
million) from adverse climate in Patagonia for conventional production. In <B>4Q24</B>, adj. EBITDA amounted to US$839&nbsp;million <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(-39%</FONT> q/q), mostly due to lower seasonal sales of gas and reduced sales price
and value of our fuels inventory, in line with international parities, and marginally, environmental provision in downstream; partially offset by growing shale oil output and recovery of conventional in Patagonia. </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:8pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>CAPEX </B>reached US$5,041&nbsp;million in <B>2024</B> <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(-5%</FONT> y/y), fully aligned with the target (US$5 billion):
63.5% was unconventional (mostly shale). <B>4Q24 </B>capex remained similar q/q at US$1,320&nbsp;million (65.3% unconventional). </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:8pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>YPF is the largest
shale oil producer of Vaca Muerta (VM)</B>: in <B>2024 </B>we averaged 122 kbbl/d (+26% y/y&nbsp;&amp; 31% of VM), in line with the target (&gt;120 kbbl/d). In <B>4Q24</B> we reached 138 kbbl/d (+10% q/q&nbsp;&amp; 32% of VM). <B>Oil exports</B>
(mainly Chile) averaged 35 kbbl/d in <B>2024</B>, (+174% y/y) and 41 kbbl/d in <B>4Q24 </B>(similar q/q). </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:8pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Vaca Muerta shale P1 reserves</B> was 854
Mboe in 2024 (+13% y/y&nbsp;&amp; 78% of total) with a reserve replacement ratio of 1.9x and average life of 8.3 years: 50% oil, 43% natural gas and 7% NGL. </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:8pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Progress on our main projects: </B></P> <P STYLE="font-size:8pt;margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt">&nbsp;</P>
<TABLE STYLE="BORDER-COLLAPSE:COLLAPSE; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:10pt" BORDER="0" CELLPADDING="0" CELLSPACING="0" WIDTH="100%">
<TR style = "page-break-inside:avoid">
<TD WIDTH="1%">&nbsp;</TD>
<TD WIDTH="2%" VALIGN="top" ALIGN="left">&#149;</TD>
<TD WIDTH="1%" VALIGN="top">&nbsp;</TD>
<TD ALIGN="left" VALIGN="top"> <P ALIGN="left" STYLE=" margin-top:0pt ; margin-bottom:0pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:10pt"><B>Mature Fields Exit Program</B><B><SUP STYLE="font-size:75%; vertical-align:top">1</SUP></B><B> </B>(50
blocks): in 2024 we collected US$136&nbsp;million from transfer of 3 blocks (Mendoza&nbsp;&amp; Chubut Prov.), plus advance payment of other blocks. After 2024, we transferred 2 more blocks (Chubut&nbsp;&amp; R&iacute;o Negro Prov.). Currently, 21
blocks are in final stages (mainly Mendoza&nbsp;&amp; Neuqu&eacute;n Prov.) and 24 blocks are in advanced ongoing negotiations (mostly Santa Cruz&nbsp;&amp; Tierra del Fuego Prov). </P></TD></TR></TABLE>
<P STYLE="font-size:6pt;margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt">&nbsp;</P>
<TABLE STYLE="BORDER-COLLAPSE:COLLAPSE; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:10pt" BORDER="0" CELLPADDING="0" CELLSPACING="0" WIDTH="100%">
<TR style = "page-break-inside:avoid">

<TD ALIGN="left" VALIGN="top"> <P ALIGN="left" STYLE=" margin-top:0pt ; margin-bottom:0pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:10pt"><B>VMOS oil export pipeline</B> (~550 kbbl/d by 2H27, ~US$3&nbsp;billion of CAPEX): YPF holds the largest stake
of 27% among the initial shippers (445 kbbl/d): YPF (120 kbbl/d). Construction started in <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">Jan-25</FONT> and we are targeting Project Finance (70% debt&nbsp;&amp; 30% equity). </P></TD></TR></TABLE>
<P STYLE="margin-top:6pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">Finally, in addition to the two int&#146;l bonds issued in 2024 (Jan: US$800&nbsp;million export-backed <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">7-y</FONT> 9.75%
yield&nbsp;&amp; Sep: US$540&nbsp;million unsecured <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">7-y</FONT> 8.75% yield), in <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">Jan-25</FONT> we issued <B>US$1.1</B><B></B><B>&nbsp;billion int&#146;l unsecured bond </B><FONT
STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(9-y</FONT> 8.5% yield), to refinance US$757&nbsp;million of Jul-25 Notes (prepaid US$315&nbsp;million in a cash tender offer and the balance in a make-whole call option in <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">Feb-25)</FONT>
and acquire 54% of Sierra Chata (VM shale gas block). </P> <P STYLE="line-height:8.0pt;margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:2pt;border-bottom:1px solid #000000;width:11%">&nbsp;</P>

<TD ALIGN="left" VALIGN="top"> <P STYLE=" margin-top:0pt ; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman; " ALIGN="left">2023 conventional lifting cost was ~US$25/BOE, while shale lifting cost was ~US$5/BOE. Also, 2023 conventional
mature fields capex was ~US$800&nbsp;million. </P></TD></TR></TABLE>

</TABLE> <P STYLE="margin-top:6pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Net Revenues</B> totaled US$19.3&nbsp;billion in <B>2024</B> (+11% y/y), mainly driven by the recovery in local fuel prices
(despite significant devaluation in <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">Dec-23),</FONT> increasing oil exports coupled with shale activity, as well as tariff increases in Metrogas (a subsidiary with functional currency in AR$, also affected by <FONT
STYLE="white-space:nowrap">Dec-23&#146;s</FONT> devaluation) and higher exports of grain and flours (2023 affected by drought), partially offset by fuel demand contraction, primarily diesel (2023 extraordinary demand with reduced price). </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:6pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">In <B>4Q24</B> net revenues decreased 10% q/q, mostly explained by lower seasonal gas sales, and decreased local fuel prices, aligned with international
parities, partially offset by a gradual recovery of local fuel demand, plus higher seasonal demand of gasoline. </P> <P STYLE="font-size:6pt;margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt">&nbsp;</P>

</TABLE> <P STYLE="margin-top:8pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">Stock variations include price effects by (US$47) million in 4Q24, US$94 million for 3Q24, (US$29) million for 4Q23, (US$111)
million in 2024 and (US$62) million in 2023. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:8pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>OPEX</B> amounted to US$7.2&nbsp;billion in <B>2024</B> (+19% y/y), primarily due the <FONT
STYLE="white-space:nowrap">Dec-23</FONT> devaluation impact in Metrogas&nbsp;&amp; AESA (AR$-functional currency), coupled with higher shale activity and negative impact of mature fields. In <B>4Q24</B>, OPEX increased 4% q/q, mainly due to an
extraordinary environmental provision in Downstream, partially offset by efficiencies gained in Downstream from logistic rearrangements. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:8pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Other
Costs</B> reached US$5.2&nbsp;billion in <B>2024</B> (stable y/y), mostly because the higher royalties (linked to hydrocarbon revenues), oil export duties and unproductive exploratory drillings were partially offset by lower depreciation and
amortization from mature fields. In <B>4Q24</B>, Other Costs rose 11% q/q, mainly driven by higher depreciation in shale assets. </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:8pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Purchases</B><B></B><B>&nbsp;&amp; Stock Variations</B> was US$4.7&nbsp;billion in <B>2024</B> <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(-10%</FONT> y/y), on the
back of a substantial decrease in fuel imports (local fuel demand contraction after price recovery, combined with economic activity decline and refining capacity expansion), partially offset by higher crude oil purchases to third parties (increased
local oil prices and processing capacity of our refineries), and to a minor extent, higher grain&nbsp;&amp; flour purchases. </P> <P STYLE="font-size:8pt;margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt">&nbsp;</P>
<P STYLE="line-height:8.0pt;margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:2pt;border-bottom:1px solid #000000;width:11%">&nbsp;</P>
<TABLE STYLE="BORDER-COLLAPSE:COLLAPSE; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:10pt" BORDER="0" CELLPADDING="0" CELLSPACING="0" WIDTH="100%">
<TR style = "page-break-inside:avoid">
<TD WIDTH="4%" VALIGN="top" ALIGN="left"><SUP STYLE="font-size:75%; vertical-align:top">2</SUP>&nbsp;</TD>
<TD ALIGN="left" VALIGN="top"> <P STYLE=" margin-top:0pt ; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman; " ALIGN="left">1 ADR = 1 share. Total issued capital stock amounted to 393,312,793 shares as of
<FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">Dec-2024</FONT> (51% Argentina Government; 32% NYSE and 17% ByMA). </P></TD></TR></TABLE>
 <p STYLE="margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt ; font-size:8pt">&nbsp;</P> <P STYLE="margin-top:0pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman" ALIGN="center">3 </P>

</DIV></Center>


<p style="margin-top:1em; margin-bottom:0em; page-break-before:always"> </p>
<HR SIZE="3" style="COLOR:#999999" WIDTH="100%" ALIGN="CENTER">


<Center><DIV STYLE="width:8.5in" align="left">

 <P STYLE="margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt" ALIGN="center">


<IMG SRC="g926270g10l58.jpg" ALT="LOGO">
 </P> <p STYLE="margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt ; font-size:8pt">&nbsp;</P>
 <P STYLE="margin-top:0pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">In <B>4Q24 </B>Purchases&nbsp;&amp; Stock Variations dropped 3% q/q, mostly due to lower seasonality in gas,
agricultural products and diesel, as well as reduced oil prices. Moreover, <B>stock variations</B> totaled <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(-US$157</FONT> million) in 4Q24, mainly explained by stock consumption of fuels and agricultural products,
combined with lower international reference prices reducing inventory value. In 3Q24, it was a gain of US$157&nbsp;million, mostly driven by higher replacement cost, coupled with higher inventory levels. </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:6pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Other operating net results</B> was <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(-US$609</FONT> million) in <B>2024</B> (vs. a gain of US$152&nbsp;million in 2023),
primarily explained by <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">one-off</FONT> costs related to mature fields (provision for operating optimizations and severance indemnities, as well as change in the fair value of assets held for sale), and marginally,
lower income from FX regime for exporters. In <B>4Q24 </B>Other operating net results sequential variation was mainly related to mature fields mentioned before. <B>Impairment </B>of PP&amp;E and inventories write-down was US$87&nbsp;million in
<B>2024</B>, compared to US$2,288&nbsp;million in 2023 (US$1,782&nbsp;million in 4Q23 related to mature fields). </P> <P STYLE="font-size:8pt;margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt">&nbsp;</P>

</TABLE> <P STYLE="margin-top:6pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Financial net results</B> recorded <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(-US$695)</FONT> million in <B>2024</B> (vs. a gain of
US$897&nbsp;million in 2023), mainly explained by the impact of the devaluation on AR$ monetary-liability position in 2023, and marginally, lower interest gains. In <B>4Q24</B> financial net results were
<FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(-US$112</FONT> million) <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(-31%</FONT> q/q), mostly due to higher gains from holding financial assets. </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:6pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">The <B>income tax </B>recorded a positive amount of US$1,2&nbsp;billion in <B>2024</B>, as devaluation was lower than inflation, resulting in lower future tax
payables. In <B>4Q24</B>, the sequential variation of <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(-US$562</FONT> million) was explained by reduced deferred income tax benefit. </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:6pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">Consequently, <B>net result</B> totaled a gain of US$2,393&nbsp;million in <B>2024</B>, compared to a loss of US$1,277&nbsp;million in 2023. In <B>4Q24</B>,
net result was a loss of US$284&nbsp;million, compared to a gain of US$1,485&nbsp;million in 3Q24. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:14pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>2. ADJ. EBITDA&nbsp;&amp; CAPEX </B></P>

<TD>&nbsp;</TD></TR>
</TABLE> <P STYLE="margin-top:8pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Revenues</B> totaled US$8.3&nbsp;billion in <B>2024</B> (+14% y/y), mainly derived from higher oil price (+9%), shale oil
expansion, and higher natural gas sales (+4% price and +2% volume), partially offset by the impact of severe weather in Patagonia on conventional production in 2024 <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(mid-Jun</FONT> &#150; <FONT
STYLE="white-space:nowrap">early-Aug).</FONT> In <B>4Q24</B> revenues decreased 12% q/q, mostly due to lower seasonal gas sales, and marginally, lower oil price (but higher oil volume). </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:8pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Lifting cost</B> reached US$15.6/BOE in <B>2024</B> (+2% y/y), mainly driven by mature fields, mostly offset by shale oil expansion. <B>Conventional </B>was
US$28.3/BOE (+13% y/y) but <B>unconventional </B>was US$5.2/BOE <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(-2%</FONT> y/y). Also, it is worth noting that excluding mature fields, total lifting cost could have been below US$9/BOE in 2024. In <B>4Q24</B>
lifting cost was US$17.3 (+7% q/q), mainly related to higher costs in real terms. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:8pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">Zooming into the lifting cost in our <B>shale core hub</B> blocks (at
100% stake), it remained similar at US$4.2/BOE in <B>2024</B>, reflecting our focus and efforts on Vaca Muerta shale oil. In <B>4Q24</B>, thanks to the high productivity and efficiencies achieved in our operations, it contracted 8% q/q, closing at a
competitive level of US$4.2/BOE. </P>
 <p STYLE="margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt ; font-size:8pt">&nbsp;</P> <P STYLE="margin-top:0pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman" ALIGN="center">5 </P>

</DIV></Center>


<p style="margin-top:1em; margin-bottom:0em; page-break-before:always"> </p>
<HR SIZE="3" style="COLOR:#999999" WIDTH="100%" ALIGN="CENTER">


<Center><DIV STYLE="width:8.5in" align="left">

 <P STYLE="margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt" ALIGN="center">


<IMG SRC="g926270g10l58.jpg" ALT="LOGO" STYLE="width:8.66875in;height:0.4875in;">
 </P> <p STYLE="margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt ; font-size:8pt">&nbsp;</P>
 <P STYLE="margin-top:0pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Royalties </B>and other taxes averaged US$6.9/BOE (+7% y/y) in <B>2024</B>, mostly due to higher crude oil
and gas prices, while in <B>4Q24</B> they were US$6.7/BOE <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(-4%</FONT> q/q), mainly due to lower seasonal natural gas price, and to a minor extent, lower crude oil price. </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Other costs</B> amounted to US$1,6&nbsp;billion in <B>2024</B> (vs. US$0.5&nbsp;billion in 2023), primarily impacted by higher costs associated to mature
fields (which occurred in 4Q24), and to a minor extent, unproductive exploratory drillings. In <B>4Q24</B>, Other costs amounted to US$839&nbsp;million (+US$541 q/q), mainly derived from the higher costs of mature fields mentioned before. </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Adj. EBITDA</B> totaled US$3.0&nbsp;billion (+8% y/y) in <B>2024</B>, mainly driven by higher hydrocarbon sales (especially shale <FONT
STYLE="white-space:nowrap">ramp-up</FONT> and increased oil price), offset by mature fields <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">~(-US$300</FONT> million) and lower conventional output due to climate impact mentioned before <FONT
STYLE="white-space:nowrap">~(-US$85</FONT> million). In <B>4Q24</B>, Adj. EBITDA was US$597&nbsp;million <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(-24%</FONT> q/q), primarily explained by lower gas seasonal sales and higher costs related to mature fields
productivity, partially offset by shale oil production <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">ramp-up</FONT> and conventional recovery from Patagonia weather. </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>CAPEX</B> amounted to US$3.7&nbsp;billion <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(-6%</FONT> y/y) in <B>2024</B>, but in the breakdown US$3.2&nbsp;billion was
allocated to unconventional (vs. US$2.5&nbsp;billion in 2023), in line with the focus of the Company in shale oil <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">ramp-up.</FONT> </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Drilling and workover activities</B> in our operated unconventional blocks recorded an outstanding performance in 2024, growing in every stage of the
horizontal well-cycle: 207 wells drilled (+14 y/y),189 wells completed (+17% y/y) and 195 wells <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">tied-in</FONT> (+29%), mostly oil wells. </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">In terms of <B>efficiencies within our unconventional operations</B>, we surpassed the efficiency targets set for 2024, averaging 308 meters per day of
drilling in our <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">core-hub</FONT> blocks (target: &gt;290), and 233 stages per set per month on fracking (target: &gt;220). It is important to mention that we achieved the highest lateral length drilling speed for one
shale well in La Angostura Sur block: 1,747 meters in 24 hours. </P> <P STYLE="font-size:8pt;margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt">&nbsp;</P>

</TABLE> <P STYLE="margin-top:8pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Crude oil production</B> in <B>2024</B> averaged 257 kbbl/d (+6% y/y), on the back of a significant growth in shale oil
(+26% y/y), which in <B>4Q24</B>, achieved a new highest mark of 138 kbbl/d (+10% q/q). This outstanding performance more than offset the contraction in conventional production, affected by the harsh winter conditions in Patagonia during almost 60
days and the natural decline of mature fields. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:8pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Natural gas production </B>grew +3% y/y in <B>2024</B>, mainly driven by the midstream expansion of the
Perito Moreno pipeline, while in <B>4Q24</B>, it declined 15% q/q, primarily due to the <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">off-peak</FONT> season. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:8pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>NGLs
production</B> remained similar y/y in <B>2024</B> at 43 kbbl/d. In <B>4Q24 </B>it dropped 29% q/q, mainly due to the maintenance in Mega facilities. </P>
 <p STYLE="margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt ; font-size:8pt">&nbsp;</P> <P STYLE="margin-top:0pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman" ALIGN="center">6 </P>

</DIV></Center>

<TD>&nbsp;</TD></TR>
</TABLE> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Proved reserves (P1)</B> closed in 2024 at 1,096 Mboe (+2% y/y), mainly driven by a solid growth of 13% in shale reserves,
partially offset by a decline in conventional fields. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">The addition of proved (developed and undeveloped) hydrocarbon reserves amounted to
220&nbsp;million barrels of oil equivalent (from table in Mboe: 248 &#150; 17 &#150; 12) on the back of the progressive developments of our unconventional operations. This result was achieved through the incorporation of 107&nbsp;million barrels of
liquids and 113 MBOE corresponding to gas reserves. Considering that reserves incorporated in 2024 exceeded the total production in the year (196 MBoe), the reserve replacement ratio (&#147;RRR&#148;) reached 1.1x with 5.6 years of reserve life.
Excluding the mature fields, RRR would have improved to 1.5x, reserves life to 6.8 years and total P1 reserves growing 7% y/y. Focusing on <B>shale reserves</B>, it scaled up to 78% of the total P1 reserves, averaging 8.3 years and RRR of 1.9x. </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">The following is a breakdown of the main variations of the addition of P1 reserves by basin: </P>
<P STYLE="font-size:6pt;margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt">&nbsp;</P>
<TABLE STYLE="BORDER-COLLAPSE:COLLAPSE; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:10pt" BORDER="0" CELLPADDING="0" CELLSPACING="0" WIDTH="100%">
<TR style = "page-break-inside:avoid">
<TD WIDTH="5%">&nbsp;</TD>
<TD WIDTH="3%" VALIGN="top" ALIGN="left">&#149;</TD>
<TD WIDTH="1%" VALIGN="top">&nbsp;</TD>
<TD ALIGN="left" VALIGN="top"> <P ALIGN="left" STYLE=" margin-top:0pt ; margin-bottom:0pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:10pt">Neuquina Basin: mainly unconventional additions from Loma La Lata Norte and Aguada del Cha&ntilde;ar blocks for
oil and Aguada Pichana Oeste and La Calera for gas </P></TD></TR></TABLE> <P STYLE="font-size:6pt;margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt">&nbsp;</P>
<TABLE STYLE="BORDER-COLLAPSE:COLLAPSE; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:10pt" BORDER="0" CELLPADDING="0" CELLSPACING="0" WIDTH="100%">
<TR style = "page-break-inside:avoid">
<TD WIDTH="5%">&nbsp;</TD>

<TD ALIGN="left" VALIGN="top"> <P ALIGN="left" STYLE=" margin-top:0pt ; margin-bottom:0pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:10pt">San Jorge Gulf Basin: incorporation of oil, primarily from Manantiales Behr block, aligned with the development
of the tertiary recovery technics </P></TD></TR></TABLE>

</TABLE> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">Stock variations include price effects by (US$41) million in 4Q24, (US$54) million in 3Q24, US$37&nbsp;million for 4Q23,
US$38&nbsp;million for 2024 and (US$164) million for 2023. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Revenues</B> totaled US$15.9&nbsp;billion in <B>2024</B> (+6% y/y), mainly due to the
recovery in local fuel price, growing crude oil exports and recovery of grain and flour exports (2023 affected by drought), partially offset by a local fuel demand contraction, particularly diesel, as well as lower local sales of fertilizers, jet
fuel and asphalts (on the back of lower volumes sold and prices). In <B>4Q24</B>, revenues slightly decreased <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(-2%</FONT> q/q), mostly driven by a downward trend in international reference prices, affecting local
fuel prices (particularly in diesel) and crude oil exports, partially offset by a gradual recovery in local fuels demand. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Refining and logistics</B>
costs were essentially flat in <B>2024</B> <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(-1%</FONT> y/y), while decreasing 2% q/q in <B>4Q24</B>. Both evolutions were benefited from efficiencies gained in Logistics and our Industrial Complexes (mainly
maintenance and energy optimization), partially offset by higher costs in real terms. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Fuel imports</B> dropped 83% y/y in <B>2024</B>, due to a
decline in local fuels demand, in line with local fuels price recovery, while 2023 was an extraordinarily high demand, coupled with a negative 20% gap to international parities. As a result, total fuel imported volumes represented 2% of our local
fuels demand in 2024 (vs. 11% in 2023). In <B>4Q24</B>, fuel imports reduction of 72% q/q was mainly related to lower diesel volume and unit cost. </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Crude oil purchases</B> (including intersegment) increased 20% y/y in <B>2024</B>, driven by an increase in purchased volumes (increasing oil exports with
shale oil <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">ramp-up</FONT> and higher refineries&#146; processing capacity), as well as higher oil prices. <B>Biofuel purchases</B> increased 11% y/y in <B>2024</B>, driven by a 32% increase in biodiesel purchases (64%
higher volumes but at 19% lower prices), partly offset by a 5% decline in bioethanol purchases (6% lower volume but at slightly higher prices). <B>Agro products purchases </B>reduced 5% y/y in <B>2024</B>, as the higher grain and flour purchased
volume was partially offset by lower fertilizers purchased volume, in line with sales. Additionally, in <B>4Q24 </B>agro producs purchases dropped 36% q/q mainly due to lower seasonal period. </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Stock variations</B> totaled <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(-US$17</FONT> million) in <B>2024</B> vs. <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(-US$147</FONT>
million) in 2023, mainly due to oil price evolution: while 2023 was a period impacted by a more pronounced drop in local oil price, in 2024 it recovered during 1H24 and gradually declined during 2H24. In <B>4Q24</B>, it was <FONT
STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(-US$147</FONT> million) vs. US$20&nbsp;million in 3Q24, mainly explained by the impact of lower international reference prices in the value of our inventories. Additionally, Other was
<FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(-US$2.4</FONT> billion) in <B>2024 </B>(+12% y/y), mainly due to higher taxes related to exports, and to a minor extent, <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(-US$58</FONT> million) of extraordinary environmental
provision. </P>
 <p STYLE="margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt ; font-size:8pt">&nbsp;</P> <P STYLE="margin-top:0pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman" ALIGN="center">8 </P>

</DIV></Center>


<p style="margin-top:1em; margin-bottom:0em; page-break-before:always"> </p>

<Center><DIV STYLE="width:8.5in" align="left">

 <P STYLE="margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt" ALIGN="center">


<IMG SRC="g926270g10l58.jpg" ALT="LOGO">
 </P> <p STYLE="margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt ; font-size:8pt">&nbsp;</P>
 <P STYLE="margin-top:0pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Adj. EBITDA,</B> excluding inventories price effect of oil products, totaled US$1,767&nbsp;million in
<B>2024</B> (+18% y/y), mainly due to fuel price recovery, combined with lower fuel imports at negative gap with international parities, partially offset by local fuel demand contraction and higher oil purchases. In <B>4Q24</B>, Adj. EBITDA dropped
15% q/q, mainly due to lower international reference prices, affecting our fuel prices, as well as the extraordinary environmental provision mentioned before. These effects were partially offset by a recovery in fuels demand and lower fuel imports.
</P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Adj. EBITDA of the Refining</B><B></B><B>&nbsp;&amp; Marketing</B> business, in unit terms, reached US$13.7/bbl in <B>2024</B>, mostly boosted by the
local fuels price recovery and efficiencies gained, compared to US$11.0/bbl reported in 2023. In <B>4Q24</B>, it was US$11.3/bbl <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(-15%</FONT> q/q), mainly explained by lower fuel prices, following international
parities. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>CAPEX</B> was US$1.1&nbsp;billion in <B>2024</B> (+3% y/y): 59% refining, 19% midstream oil, 17% logistics and 5% others. </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">In our <B>refineries,</B> during <B>2024</B> CAPEX was allocated in the following main projects: </P>

revamping of existing gasoline units at La Plata refinery, already completed in 2Q4, as well as the construction of a new diesel oil hydrotreatment unit at Luj&aacute;n de Cuyo refinery, expecting in 4Q25 </P></TD></TR></TABLE>

 <p STYLE="margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt ; font-size:8pt">&nbsp;</P> <P STYLE="margin-top:0pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman" ALIGN="center">9 </P>

</DIV></Center>


<p style="margin-top:1em; margin-bottom:0em; page-break-before:always"> </p>
<HR SIZE="3" style="COLOR:#999999" WIDTH="100%" ALIGN="CENTER">


<Center><DIV STYLE="width:8.5in" align="left">

 <P STYLE="margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt" ALIGN="center">


<IMG SRC="g926270g10l58.jpg" ALT="LOGO">
 </P> <p STYLE="margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt ; font-size:8pt">&nbsp;</P>
 <P STYLE="margin-top:0pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Crude oil processed </B>averaged 301 Kbbl/d in <B>2024</B> (+2% y/y), recording a refinery utilization of
89%, mainly driven by the revamping of Topping D at La Plata Refinery completed in 4Q23, which enabled to increase processing capacity levels, combined with the completion of additional works in 2024, within the New Fuels Specifications Project
framework, as well as an increase in oil pumping capacity from Puesto Hernandez to Luj&aacute;n de Cuyo Complex during 2024. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Domestic fuels sales
volumes</B> declined by 7% y/y in <B>2024</B>, on the back of fuels price recovery and given the fact that 2023 was impacted by an extraordinary demand driven by reduced prices. During 2H24, fuels demand started to slightly recover, increasing 4%
q/q in <B>4Q24</B>, mostly boosted by gasoline demand. In this context, we managed to maintain our fuel sales market share at strong levels, posting 56% in <B>2024</B>. </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Petrochemicals volumes</B> grew 14% y/y in <B>2024</B>, due to higher foreign demand, led by methanol exports, partially offset by lower local demand. </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Fertilizers, grain and flours sales volumes</B> increased 26% y/y in <B>2024</B>, on the back of a substantial recovery of grain and flour sales in the
export market, also considering that 2023 was affected by a severe drought in Argentina. This growth was partially offset by lower fertilizers and agrochemical sales in the local market. </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Net average fuels prices in the domestic market measured in dollar terms </B>expanded by 13% y/y in <B>2024</B>, driven by an active pricing adjustment to
converge with international parities. As a result, our gap to import parities narrowed significantly, from 20% in 2023 to only 2% in 2024, despite the significant devaluation that took place in December 2023. </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Prices for petrochemical</B><B></B><B>&nbsp;&amp; other refined products</B> contracted 9% y/y in <B>2024</B>, aligned with a downward trend in
international prices of petrochemical and certain refined products, such as jet fuel, fuel oil and coal. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:18pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>3.3 GAS&nbsp;&amp; POWER </B></P>

</TABLE> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Revenues</B> totaled US$3,015&nbsp;million in <B>2024</B> (+27% y/y), mainly due to higher sales from our subsidiary
Metrogas, as distributor, which was impacted by the sharp devaluation of <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">Dec-23</FONT> (since its functional currency is in AR$), higher volumes sold and adjustment tariffs during 2024. This was also combined with
higher natural gas sales, as producer and increasing midstream gas revenues. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Natural gas purchases</B> increased 12% y/y in <B>2024</B>, mostly driven
by higher purchases from Metrogas, in line with increased natural gas sales (higher volumes and prices), while <B>operating costs</B><B></B><B>&nbsp;&amp; other</B> rose 68% y/y, mainly due to the local currency devaluation impact on our subsidiary
Metrogas mentioned before. Additionally, we recognized a charge for doubtful sales receivables for US$51&nbsp;million in 2024<SUP STYLE="font-size:75%; vertical-align:top">3</SUP>, mostly owed by CAMMESA. </P>
<P STYLE="line-height:8.0pt;margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:2pt;border-bottom:1px solid #000000;width:11%">&nbsp;</P>
<TABLE STYLE="BORDER-COLLAPSE:COLLAPSE; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:10pt" BORDER="0" CELLPADDING="0" CELLSPACING="0" WIDTH="100%">
<TR style = "page-break-inside:avoid">
<TD WIDTH="4%" VALIGN="top" ALIGN="left"><SUP STYLE="font-size:75%; vertical-align:top">3</SUP>&nbsp;</TD>
<TD ALIGN="left" VALIGN="top"> <P STYLE=" margin-top:0pt ; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman; " ALIGN="left">According to Resolution N&deg; 58/24 of the Secretary of Energy, released on May&nbsp;8, 2024, the Company
recognized a charge for doubtful sales receivables within operating costs of US$22&nbsp;million in 2Q24 and US$29&nbsp;million in 1Q24, mainly owed by CAMMESA. </P></TD></TR></TABLE>
 <p STYLE="margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt ; font-size:8pt">&nbsp;</P> <P STYLE="margin-top:0pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman" ALIGN="center">10 </P>

</DIV></Center>


<p style="margin-top:1em; margin-bottom:0em; page-break-before:always"> </p>
<HR SIZE="3" style="COLOR:#999999" WIDTH="100%" ALIGN="CENTER">


<Center><DIV STYLE="width:8.5in" align="left">

 <P STYLE="margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt" ALIGN="center">


<IMG SRC="g926270g10l58.jpg" ALT="LOGO">
 </P> <p STYLE="margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt ; font-size:8pt">&nbsp;</P>
 <P STYLE="margin-top:0pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">As a result of the aforementioned factors, <B>Adjusted EBITDA</B> totaled US$207&nbsp;million in <B>2024</B>
(vs. US$43&nbsp;million in 2023). In <B>4Q24</B>, it decreased 75% q/q, mainly due to lower seasonal natural gas sales. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>CAPEX</B> totaled US$
146&nbsp;million <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(-18%</FONT> vs. 2023) in <B>2024</B>. During <B>4Q24</B>, CAPEX was focused on: </P> <P STYLE="font-size:6pt;margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt">&nbsp;</P>
<TABLE STYLE="BORDER-COLLAPSE:COLLAPSE; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:10pt" BORDER="0" CELLPADDING="0" CELLSPACING="0" WIDTH="100%">
<TR style = "page-break-inside:avoid">
<TD WIDTH="5%">&nbsp;</TD>
<TD WIDTH="3%" VALIGN="top" ALIGN="left">&#149;</TD>
<TD WIDTH="1%" VALIGN="top">&nbsp;</TD>
<TD ALIGN="left" VALIGN="top"> <P ALIGN="left" STYLE=" margin-top:0pt ; margin-bottom:0pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:10pt"><B>Fully Revamping</B> of the natural gas treatment plant at Loma Negra, aiming to increase the current
processing capacity </P></TD></TR></TABLE> <P STYLE="font-size:6pt;margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt">&nbsp;</P>
<TABLE STYLE="BORDER-COLLAPSE:COLLAPSE; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:10pt" BORDER="0" CELLPADDING="0" CELLSPACING="0" WIDTH="100%">
<TR style = "page-break-inside:avoid">
<TD WIDTH="5%">&nbsp;</TD>
<TD WIDTH="3%" VALIGN="top" ALIGN="left">&#149;</TD>
<TD WIDTH="1%" VALIGN="top">&nbsp;</TD>
<TD ALIGN="left" VALIGN="top"> <P ALIGN="left" STYLE=" margin-top:0pt ; margin-bottom:0pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:10pt"><B>Argentine LNG project</B>: in <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">Dec-24,</FONT> we signed a Project Development
Agreement with Shell, which joined the project as our new strategic partner </P></TD></TR></TABLE> <P STYLE="font-size:6pt;margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt">&nbsp;</P>
<TABLE STYLE="BORDER-COLLAPSE:COLLAPSE; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:10pt" BORDER="0" CELLPADDING="0" CELLSPACING="0" WIDTH="100%">
<TR style = "page-break-inside:avoid">
<TD WIDTH="5%">&nbsp;</TD>
<TD WIDTH="3%" VALIGN="top" ALIGN="left">&#149;</TD>
<TD WIDTH="1%" VALIGN="top">&nbsp;</TD>
<TD ALIGN="left" VALIGN="top"> <P ALIGN="left" STYLE=" margin-top:0pt ; margin-bottom:0pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:10pt"><B>South Hub gathering project</B>: mainly to expand gas processing capacity at Sierra Barrosa treatment plants;
we already completed the first stage in 2024, and plan to launch a second phase in 2025 </P></TD></TR></TABLE> <P STYLE="font-size:6pt;margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt">&nbsp;</P>

with El Port&oacute;n Industrial Complex, expecting to be operational by 2027 </P></TD></TR></TABLE> <P STYLE="margin-top:18pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>4. LIQUIDITY AND SOURCES OF CAPITAL </B></P>

</TABLE> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">FCF = Cash flow from Operations less capex (Investing activities), M&amp;A (Investing activities), and interest and leasing
payments (Financing activities). </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">The <B>free cash flow</B> for <B>2024</B> was negative at U$760&nbsp;million, mainly due to the impact of <FONT
STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(-US$433</FONT> million) from mature fields: (1) <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">~(-US$300</FONT> million) in adj. EBITDA; (2) <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(-US$269</FONT> million) of additions of assets held for
sale, in net cash flow from investing activities; and (3)&nbsp;US$136&nbsp;million of proceeds from transfer of blocks and advance payments received. Also, in 2024, it was impacted by the climate contingency in Patagonia <FONT
STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(-US$85</FONT> million), coupled with temporary deferred import liabilities from 2023 paid in 2024 <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(-US$166</FONT> million), collected lower dividends from affiliates and paid higher debt
service payments. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">In <B>4Q24</B> free cash flow shifted into positive ground at US$64&nbsp;million. While CAPEX was not fully compensated by Adj. EBITDA,
<B>4Q24</B> was positively affected mainly by collection of overdue natural gas receivables and proceeds from certain mature fields, in addition to lower debt service payment. </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">In terms of liquidity, our <B>cash and short-term investments</B> increased to US$1,508&nbsp;million by the end of December 2024, growing 9% vs. 2023. </P>

<P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">As of December&nbsp;31, 2024, YPF&#146;s <B>consolidated net debt</B> totaled US$7.4&nbsp;billion, similar q/q but increasing 9% y/y, while the adj. EBITDA
recorded a solid growth in 2024 (+15% vs. 2023), reducing <B>net leverage ratio</B> from 1.7x in 2023 to 1.6x in 2024. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">In terms of <B>financing</B>, in
4Q24 we issued two <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">4-year</FONT> new dollar-denominated local bonds for a total of US$150 million: US$125&nbsp;million MEP and US$25&nbsp;million CCL, at a 6.5% and 7.0% yield, respectively. Moreover, we reopened the
<FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">3-year</FONT> syndicated loan (secured in 3Q24) with local banks, for additional US$100&nbsp;million and secured around US$60&nbsp;million in short term trade financing facilities. </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">By the beginning of 2025, we issued a <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">9-year</FONT> unsecured international bond for US$1.1&nbsp;billion at a yield of 8.50%.
The proceeds were disbursed to refinance 2025 Notes (US$ 757 million) and acquire 54.45% of Sierra Chata shale gas block in Vaca Muerta. Regarding the 2025 Notes, we executed a cash tender offer, prepaying US$315&nbsp;million in <FONT
STYLE="white-space:nowrap">Jan-25,</FONT> while executing &#147;make-whole&#148; call option for the balance in <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">Feb-25.</FONT> </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">Lastly, after 4Q24, we issued two new local bonds denominated in &#147;Dollar MEP&#148; for US$200 million: </P>
<P STYLE="font-size:6pt;margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt">&nbsp;</P>
<TABLE STYLE="BORDER-COLLAPSE:COLLAPSE; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:10pt" BORDER="0" CELLPADDING="0" CELLSPACING="0" WIDTH="100%">

with a stable outlook) and S&amp;P (from CCC to B+). </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">Regarding our <B>maturity profile</B>, the Company faces debt maturities for the 12 months of 2025
for less than US$1&nbsp;billion, mainly concentrated in: US$400&nbsp;million (short-term local and international trade facilities), US$282 of international bonds&#146; amortizations, US$147&nbsp;million of local bond and US$51&nbsp;million with CAF.

<P STYLE="margin-top:18pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>6. ABOUT YPF </B></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:6pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">YPF is the largest energy company in
Argentina, fully integrated in the oil and gas value chain. Our main businesses are: (i)&nbsp;in the upstream, we produce ~40% and ~30% of the country&#146;s oil and gas, respectively, and we are the largest shale producer in Vaca Muerta, in process
of divestment of conventional mature fields; (ii)&nbsp;in the downstream, we operate 3 refineries (~50% of Argentina&#146;s refining capacity) and lead the local diesel and gasoline sales (market share &gt;55%); and (iii)&nbsp;in gas and power,
Metrogas, our subsidiary, distributes ~25% of the country&#146;s natural gas, while YPF Luz, our affiliate, is the third largest power generation company in Argentina. The Government is the controlling shareholder with a 51% stake, and YPF is listed
in the NYSE and ByMA. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:18pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>7. DISCLAIMER </B></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:6pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">Additional
information about YPF S.A., a sociedad an&oacute;nima organized under the laws of Argentina (the &#147;Company&#148; or &#147;YPF&#148;) can be found in the &#147;Investors&#148; section on the website at <U>www.ypf.com</U>. </P>
